2,1,1,2,1,1,1,1,1,1,27,1,1,1,1,1,126,6: Type: 50007 (ListItem) Name: "Rationale and Key Findings: "John J. Horton (2023) established the theoretical framework for this domain in Large Language Models as Simulated Economic Agents. Horton proposes that LLMs act as 'implicit computational models of humans.' When placed in economic scenarios, they do not behave as profit-maximizing algorithms (Homo Economicus) but rather simulate the social preferences, fairness norms, and trust dynamics present in their human training data."" 2,1,1,2,1,1,1,1,1,1,27,1,1,1,1,1,211: Type: 50020 (Text) Name: "DeepSeek-R1, released in early 2025, represents a significant breakthrough in the democratization of high-level reasoning. Unlike many predecessors that relied heavily on Supervised Fine-Tuning (SFT) to mimic reasoning patterns, DeepSeek-R1 was trained via large-scale Reinforcement Learning (RL) specifically incentivized to develop reasoning capabilities." LocalizedType: "text"
2,1,1,2,1,1,1,1,1,1,27,1,1,1,1,1,212: Type: 50020 (Text) Name: "This training methodology—"pure reinforcement learning"—allows the model to exhibit emergent behaviors such as self-verification, reflection, and the generation of long Chain-of-Thought (CoT) trajectories without needing human-labeled reasoning data. Technical reports indicate that DeepSeek-R1 achieves performance comparable to, and in some specific benchmarks (such as AIME 2024 and MATH-500) exceeding, proprietary models like OpenAI's o1." 2,1,1,2,1,1,1,1,1,1,27,1,1,1,1,1,233: Type: 50020 (Text) Name: "Key Insight" LocalizedType: "text"

For example, an agent might struggle to "select the red dress with the floral pattern" if the text description is generic, requiring it to visually discriminate between options. This limitation highlights a critical nuance for the dissertation: while functional usability can be automated, visual usability evaluation still requires the most advanced (and expensive) multimodal models, and even then, validation against human ground truth is recommended." 2,1,1,2,1,1,1,1,1,1,27,1,1,1,1,1,469: Type: 50020 (Text) Name: "The epistemological foundations of social science are currently undergoing a seismic shift, precipitated by the emergence of Large Language Models (LLMs) and their evolution into "Agentic AI"—computational entities capable of perception, memory, reflection, and goal-directed behavior. For nearly a century, the measurement of human sentiment, social cohesion, and trust has relied on the direct interrogation of biological subjects through instruments such as the General Social Survey (GSS) and the World Values Survey (WVS). These methods, while foundational, are plagued by escalating costs, declining response rates, and the inherent difficulties of reaching marginalized or specific demographic subpopulations." 2,1,1,2,1,1,1,1,1,1,27,1,1,1,1,1,475: Type: 50020 (Text) Name: "The premise driving this "Silicon Sampling" revolution is that LLMs, having been trained on a massive corpus of human internet discourse (the Common Crawl), have encoded not merely the syntax of human language but the latent distribution of human attitudes, values, and biases. The critical research question, therefore, is whether these models can be prompted—or architected as agents—to accurately simulate the trust profiles of specific human populations, thereby serving as high-fidelity proxies for social research." We explore the architectural distinctions between simple personas and generative agents, the fidelity of survey replication, the "hyper-trusting" anomalies observed in economic games, and the pervasive "WEIRD" (Western, Educated, Industrialized, Rich, Democratic) biases that threaten the global validity of synthetic social research." This distinction is not merely technical but fundamental to the nature of simulated trust, as trust is arguably a function of memory and cumulative experience." LocalizedType: "text"

2,1,1,2,1,1,1,1,1,1,27,1,1,1,1,1,484: Type: 50020 (Text) Name: "In the seminal work "Out of One, Many: Using Language Models to Simulate Human Samples" (2023), Argyle et al. demonstrated that LLMs are not monolithic in their opinions but contain a "multiverse" of latent personas. 2,1,1,2,1,1,1,1,1,1,27,1,1,1,1,1,521: Type: 50020 (Text) Name: "A subtle but pervasive influence on these survey results is Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback (RLHF). Models are tuned to be "helpful, harmless, and honest." This safety tuning appears to introduce a "pro-social bias" into trust questions. When an AI is asked if people can be trusted, the safety-aligned response—often reflecting a normative ideal of social harmony—is to affirm trust or provide a balanced, optimistic view. This creates a systematic artifact where synthetic samples may appear more trusting than the jagged, cynical reality of the human populations they mimic." LocalizedType: "text"
